2. At the outset, the Chairman welcomed the Members of the Committee and 
informed that the Secretary, Department of Health and Family Welfare, and other 
officials have been invited in connection with the consideration of Demands for 
Grants (2021-22) of the Department of Health & Family Welfare ( Health Sector 
and NHM). The Chairman welcoming the Secretary and other officials of the 
Ministry desired to know whether the allocation made to the Department was 
sufficient to meet their requirements . He further inter alia asked the Secretary to 
focus dur ing his presentation on the projected demand, actual allocation made , 
shortage of funds for schemes , if any, and further requirement of the Department. 
He then asked the Secretary to introduce the team of officials before making his 
presentation. 
3. Thereafter, the Committee heard the presentation of Secretary Department of 
Health and Family Welfare. The Secretary explained that in this year’s Budget 
document, the expenditure part combines the expenditure of the Department of 
Health and Family Welfare, Department of Health Research, Ministry of AYUSH, 
Department of Drinking Water and Sanitation as well as Nutrition because all these 
cumulatively impinge on human health.  So, the present budget does not only focus 
on the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare,  but also looks at the promotive, 
curative and preventive aspects of health.  While giving a brief overview on the 
Demand for Grants (2021 -22) of the Health Sector and National Health Mission, 
the Secretary informed the Committee that the Ministry is gettin g funds under two 
Demand Heads i.e.  Rs.71, 269 crores under Demand No.44 and Rs.35,000 crores 
for COVID vaccination under the Demand  No.  40.  Secondly , he emphasized on 
the fact that last three-four years' Budgetary allocations to the Ministry of Health as 
well as its utilization shows that that the Ministry has been able to spent more than 
95 per cent, at times, even 100 per cent of w hatever has been allocated to it .  He 

196 
 
also submitted to the Committee that although the Ministry has got increased 
allocation, it was hoping for a slightly higher allocation in the present financial 
year.  
4.  The Secretary also apprised the Committee about the s tatus of the ongoing 
vaccination drive. The Secretary submitted that though the transmission rate is 
high, the mortality rate in India due to Covid -19 is low when compared to the 
corresponding global figure. He further submitted that the travel advisories have 
been issued and implemented at airports for international flights. People with fever 
and other symptoms are being quarantined and tested for Corona Virus.   
5. Thereafter Additional Secretary and Financial Advisor (AS&FA) of the 
Department informed the Committee about the major initiatives and activities of 
the Department and inter alia highlighted several initiatives like PM Atma Nirbhar 
Swasth Bharat Yojana for holistic a pproach to Healthcare in the country. He gave 
an exhaustive insight into the t rends of budgetary allocation and expenditure 
pattern of the Ministry in previous years and also the projected de mand and the 
budgetary allocations to the Ministry for the financial year 2021 -22. Citing 
Economic Survey 2020 -21, he further  emphasized on the need of more p ublic 
investment in healthcare to reduce Out of Pocket Expenditure (OOPE).  
6.  The Committee was then informed about the Covid situation in the country 
as on 15th February; 2021 and measures taken for augmenting infrastructure of 
hospitals and testing labs and about IT interventions, research work and 
collaborations for Vaccine development. 
7.  Additional Secretary and Mission Director ( AS & MD ) (NHM) informed  
about the ongoing status of various Central Sector Health Schemes like Ayushman 
Bharat – Pradhan Mantri Jan Arogya Yojana (AB -PMJAY), Pradhan Mantri 
Swasthya Suraksha Yojana (PMSSY), Kayakalp Scheme, Swastha Nagrik Abhiyan 

197 
 
along with the important  “ END TB by 2025 – TB Harega Desh Jeetega ” to 
contain TB in the country . She further stated the measures taken by the Ministry 
for strengthening of States’ Drug Regulatory System and for making Food Safety 
Standard Authority of India more potent to contain adulteration of food products in 
the country.  
8.  During the course of the meeting, Members raised the following queries:- 
i. Difference between the projected demand and the budgetary allocation to the 
Ministry;

epidemics/pandemics.

detail of the number of volunteers who participated in Covaxin's trials;

xii. Status of AIIMS Madurai.  
xiii. Steps required to be taken to address the problem of health care  
xiv. Need for starting/extension of Ayushman Bharat Yojna to all; 
xv. Need of Integrated Medicine Course  lnfidulni ulnnieinc diulflni zlv .
Allopathic, Homeopathy, Ayurveda.

IAS, IPS etc.
